Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n    10→\n    11→2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n    12→\n    13→3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n    14→\n    15→---\n    16→\n    17→## Project Context\n    18→\n    19→- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n    20→- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n    21→- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n    22→- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n    23→\n    24→## Reference Information\n    25→\n    26→- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n    27→- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n    28→- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n    29→- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n    30→- **Documentation** on nwb file format is at https://nwb-schema.readthedocs.io/en/latest/\n    31→\n    32→## Decoder Task\n    33→\n    34→Temporally align based on start of the trial. \n    35→\n    36→### Decoder Inputs:\n    37→- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n    38→- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n    39→- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n    40→- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n    41→\n    42→### Decoder Outputs\n    43→- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n    44→  - 0:  < -50 cm\n    45→  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n    46→  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n    47→  - 3: 0 cm\n    48→  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n    49→  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n    50→  - 6: > +50 cm\n    51→- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n    52→- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n    53→  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n    54→  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n    55→  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n    56→  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n    57→  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n    58→- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    59→- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n    60→- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    61→\n    62→## Target Data Format\n    63→\n    64→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    65→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    66→\n    67→```python\n    68→data = {\n    69→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    70→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    71→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    72→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    73→            ...\n    74→        ],\n    75→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    76→            ...\n    77→        ],\n    78→        ...\n    79→    ],\n    80→\n    81→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    82→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    83→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    84→            ...\n    85→        ],\n    86→        ...\n    87→    ],\n    88→\n    89→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    90→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    91→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    92→            ...\n    93→        ],\n    94→        ...\n    95→    ],\n    96→\n    97→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    98→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    99→\n   100→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n   101→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n   102→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n   103→    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n   104→\n   105→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n   106→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n   107→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n   108→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n   109→        ...\n   110→    ],\n   111→\n   112→    'metadata': {\n   113→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   114→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   115→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   116→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   117→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   118→        ...\n   119→    }\n   120→}\n   121→```\n   122→\n   123→### Key Format Requirements\n   124→\n   125→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   126→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   127→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   128→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   129→\n   130→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   131→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   132→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   133→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   134→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   135→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   136→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   137→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   138→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   139→\n   140→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   141→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   142→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   143→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   144→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   145→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   146→\n   147→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   148→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   149→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   150→\n   151→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   152→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   153→\n   154→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   155→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   156→\n   157→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   158→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   159→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   160→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   161→\n   162→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   163→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   164→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   165→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   166→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   167→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   168→\n   169→## Python environment\n   170→\n   171→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   172→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   173→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   174→\n   175→## Decoder Reference\n   176→\n   177→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   178→- Optional arguments:\n   179→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   180→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   181→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   182→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   183→\n   184→### Purpose\n   185→\n   186→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   187→1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   188→2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   189→3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n   190→4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n   191→5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n   192→\n   193→### Consistency requirements\n   194→\n   195→Your processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n   196→- Loading of data\n   197→- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n   198→- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n   199→- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n   200→\n   201→To check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n   202→\n   203→Whenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n   204→\n   205→Discrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n   206→\n   207→Carefully verify your work after every step. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code. \n   210→\n   211→---\n   212→\n   213→## Conversion Workflow\n   214→\n   215→**IMPORTANT**: Follow these steps in order. Experimental and behavioral variables\n   313→  - Data processing\n   314→  - Data curation\n   315→- Iterate until your understanding of the reference code, dataset, and reference texts are consistent\n   316→\n   317→**Done when**: You have resolved all discrepancies and documented your final understanding in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 4\".\n   318→\n   319→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 5, update Step 4 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   320→\n   321→---\n   322→\n   323→### Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   324→\n   325→**Goal**: Create a detailed plan for mapping source data to target format.\n   326→\n   327→**Actions**:\n   328→- Map source data elements to target format components:\n   329→  - Which variables contain neural activity?\n   330→  - Identify ALL available experimental variables\n   331→  - Which variables should be decoder **inputs** vs **outputs**? (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   332→  - How to discretize continuous outputs (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   333→  - Which inputs/outputs should be **time-varying** vs **per-trial** (Follow Decoder Task section)\n   334→  - How does the reference code load and manipulate this data?\n   335→  - When possible, import or copy code from the reference code in `code` directory. \n   336→- Consult CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4 and reference materials to determine:\n   337→  - Temporal binning for spike rates (if applicable)\n   338→  - Temporal alignment of trials\n   339→  - Data filtering and curation rules for neurons, trials, and sessions\n   340→  - Brain regions recorded from\n   341→- Mapping should be consistent with CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Steps 1-4.\n   342→- Check for variables indicating valid data periods — exclude invalid data\n   343→- Note all decisions, edge cases, and data quality issues\n   344→- Plan and document **sanity and consistency checks** based on dataset size information from the reference texts, code, and data\n   345→\n   346→**Done when**:\n   347→- You have a complete mapping document in CONVERSION_NOTES.md specifying every source-to-target variable mapping under \"Step 5\"\n   348→- You have documented all decisions under \"Step 5\"\n   349→- You have documented sanity and consistency checks under \"Step 5\"\n   350→\n   351→**⚠️ CHECKPOINT**: Before proceeding to Step 6, update Step 5 CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   352→\n   353→---\n   354→\n   355→### Step 6: Script Development\n   356→\n   357→**Goal**: Write the conversion script to `convert_data.py`.\n   358→\n   359→**Actions**:\n   360→- Write all conversion code in the file `convert_data.py`\n   361→- Script should run as **`python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`**\n   362→- Include options:\n   363→  - `--full` (default): Process all sessions\n   364→  - `--sample`: Process only 2 sessions for testing\n   365→  - `--show-processing`: Plot visualizations of EVERY processing step for up to 2 sessions. Save plots as `processing_<session_id>.png`.\n   366→- Reuse code from reference code bases where appropriate\n   367→- Write efficient code:\n   368→  - Vectorize loops\n   369→  - Avoid unnecessary file I/O\n   370→  - Use parallel processing if beneficial\n   371→  - Print timing information to find bottlenecks\n   372→- Plots for `--show-processing` mode should visually convince the user that every step of the conversion is correct.\n   373→  - There are no temporal misalignments\n   374→  - Discretization of continuous outputs is correct\n   375→- Write clear, well-documented functions\n   376→- Handle missing data appropriately (consult references, use sensible defaults, document)\n   377→- Validate data shapes and types at each step\n   378→- Include sanity checks (e.g., trial counts match across arrays)\n   379→\n   380→**Done when**: `convert_data.py` exists and runs efficiently without errors.\n   381→\n   382→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 7 until `convert_data.py` exists and you have updated Step 6 in CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n   383→\n   384→---\n   385→\n   386→### Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   387→\n   388→**Goal**: Validate the conversion on a small sample before processing the full dataset.\n   389→\n   390→**Actions**:\n   391→1. Run `python -u convert_data.py sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee conversion_sample_out.txt`\n   392→  a. Verify the sample data structure matches the specification.\n   393→  b. Check dimensions are consistent across trials/sessions.\n   394→  c. Check that no data is missed during conversion. \n   395→  d. Validate that metadata accurately describes the data.\n   396→2. Improve efficiency of conversion.\n   397→  a. Look at and note timing information for the conversion. \n   398→  b. Estimate how long the full conversion will run. When you do this, make sure that your estimate accounts for differences in lengths/numbers of trials/sessions. \n   399→  c. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   400→3. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_sample_out.txt`.\n   401→4. Analyze the output file `verification_sample_out.txt`:\n   402→  a. Verify no errors reported.\n   403→  b. Attempt to address any warnings\n   404→  c. Check input ranges and output value distributions against expectations from reference texts.\n   405→\n   406→**Done when**: \n   407→- `sample_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   408→- `verification_sample_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   409→- Processing plots show no anomalies\n   410→- Document sample statistics in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 7\"\n   411→\n   412→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 8 until `sample_data.pkl` and `verification_sample_out.txt` files exist, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 7 is filled in. \n   413→\n   414→---\n   415→\n   416→### Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   417→\n   418→**Goal**: Verify the decoder runs successfully on sample data.\n   419→\n   420→**Actions**:\n   421→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py sample_data.pkl 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_sample_out.txt`\n   422→2. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   423→3. **Check accuracy**: Verify accuracy is above chance for EVERY output\n   424→\n   425→**Investigate any issues**:\n   426→- If accuracy is low: check for conversion bugs or reconsider output representation\n   427→\n   428→**Done when**: \n   429→- Decoder training completes without errors\n   430→- Loss decreases over epochs\n   431→- Accuracies are above chance\n   432→- Document all results in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 8\"\n   433→\n   434→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 9 until `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` file exists, and CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 8 is filled in. \n   435→\n   436→---\n   437→\n   438→### Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   439→\n   440→**Goal**: Process the complete dataset.\n   441→\n   442→**Actions**:\n   443→1. Review your time estimate from Step 7.\n   444→2. If estimated time is long (e.g. longer than 15 minutes), optimize bottlenecks first.\n   445→3. Run `python -u convert_data.py converted_data.pkl --full 2>&1 | tee conversion_full_out.txt`\n   446→4. As the code runs, update your estimates of how long processing will take. If it is much longer than your previous estimate (> 1.5x), kill the process, optimize bottlenecks, and repeat. \n   447→5. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee verification_full_out.txt`. \n   448→6. Check that no data was lost during conversion \n   449→7. Spot-check a few sessions to verify data integrity\n   450→8. **Check for consistency** between dataset statistics in `verification_full_out.txt` and the reference texts\n   451→9. Investigate any inconsistencies, and revise the conversion script until all dataset statistics are consistent\n   452→\n   453→**Done when**:\n   454→- `converted_data.pkl` is created and passes manual inspection\n   455→- `verification_full_out.txt` is created and passes inspection\n   456→- **ALL** dataset statistics are consistent with values from reference texts\n   457→- You have documented statistics and consistency in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 9\"\n   458→\n   459→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 10 until `converted_data.pkl` and `verification_full_out.txt` files exist and CONVERSION_NOTES.MD Step 9 is filled in.\n   460→\n   461→---\n   462→\n   463→### Step 10: Critical Review 1\n   464→\n   465→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain.\n   466→\n   467→**Check 1: Output log verification**\n   468→Read through `verification_full_out.txt` to find and fix **all errors**. Address each warning by either fixing its cause or verifying it that it is not possible to fix it. Explain why each warning could not be fixed in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   469→\n   470→**Check 2: Construct sanity checks**\n   471→- Develop at least one sanity check each on neural, input, and output data.\n   472→- These must involve loading in the original data files (NOT through your conversion code).\n   473→- Construct comparison tests and criteria using `np.allclose()`. \n   474→- These tests can be spot-checks of the data, e.g. neural activity at trial 5, timepoint 10, neuron 3. \n   475→- Fix all mismatches/sanity check failures. \n   476→- Document your sanity checks to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   477→\n   478→**Check 3: Reference code comparison**\n   479→- Compare your code to the reference code and methods. \n   480→- For each major processing step — (a) data loading, (b) neuron/trial filtering, (c) temporal alignment, (d) binning, (e) input construction, (f) output construction — identify the corresponding code in your script and in the reference, and compare the logic. \n   481→- Check whether you use the same variables, filtering, and processing as the reference code. \n   482→- Fix any mismatches. \n   483→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   484→- Explain your reasoning for any differences in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`.\n   485→\n   486→**Check 4: Key statistics comparison**\n   487→- Verify that key statistics (number of animals, sessions, trials, neurons, input and output ranges, output distributions) match information available in the reference paper. \n   488→- Write code to investigate any discrepancies. \n   489→- Fix any discovered issues. \n   490→- Document your comparisons in `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`. \n   491→\n   492→**Check 5: Check for edge cases**\n   493→- Check for off-by-one errors at the beginning/ends of trials, sessions, and subjects, or in definitions of the inputs and outputs. \n   494→- There may be minor issues in the data that your code must handle. Check that your code robustly handles issues in the original data. \n   495→\n   496→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   497→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   498→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   499→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   500→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   501→\n   502→Write a report to CONVERSION_NOTES.md describing **every** check you did, to help convince the user that the conversion code works. **Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 10\".\n   503→\n   504→**⚠️ DO NOT PROCEED** to Step 11 until you have checked for consistency between **every** statistic in the reference texts, code, and data and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under Step 10. \n   505→\n   506→---\n   507→\n   508→### Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   509→\n   510→**Goal**: Validate the decoder on the **complete** dataset.\n   511→\n   512→**⚠️ IMPORTANT**: This step must complete fully. Do not skip or abbreviate. Do not proceed to Step 12 until this step is completely finished.\n   513→\n   514→**Actions**:\n   515→1. Run `python -u train_decoder.py converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee train_decoder_full_out.txt`. If the GPU does not have sufficient RAM for the network training, use the flag `--cpu` to specify to use the CPU to train. \n   516→2. **Wait for complete execution** — this may take significant time for large datasets\n   517→3. **Check training**: Verify loss decreases over epochs\n   518→4. **Check accuracy**: High accuracy is an indicator that data conversion has been done correctly. Accuracy near chance is a sign that there might be issues in temporal alignment of signals, choice of data streams, or filtering or processing of data. Compare accuracy to expectations based on the paper. \n   519→\n   520→**Done when**: \n   521→1. Full decoder training completes (the script finishes running)\n   522→2. Accuracy results are documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 11\" with a table of accuracies\n   523→\n   524→---\n   525→\n   526→### Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   527→\n   528→**Goal**: Find and fix any errors by performing the following checks. Fix issues and re-run affected steps. Iterate until no issues remain. Perform all of the following checks:\n   529→\n   530→**Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis**\n   531→- For each output, compare validation accuracy to chance (1/num_classes)\n   532→- Accuracy below chance indicates a bug, investigate and fix conversion issues to improve this. \n   533→- Accuracy below 1.5x chance may also indicate a bug, investigate whether changes in conversion logic could improve accuracy. \n   534→\n   535→**Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper**\n   536→- Find every decoding accuracy reported in the reference paper\n   537→- Create a table comparing your accuracy to the paper's accuracy for each variable\n   538→- If your accuracy is lower than the reported accuracy, this may indicate a bug in your conversion that you must fix. \n   539→- Differences in algorithm/architecture should be relatively small because of the data dimensionality and size. Do not use this as an excuse to dismiss differences, investigate them completely before moving on. \n   540→\n   541→**Check 3: Train vs validation gap**\n   542→- If training accuracy is much higher (>1.5x) than validation accuracy for any output, investigate overfitting or data leakage. \n   543→\n   544→**If accuracy is low for an output, try these specific debugging steps:**\n   545→1. Verify the output values are correct by loading raw data and checking 3 specific trials\n   546→2. Check temporal alignment — plot neural + output for a single trial to verify they're synchronized\n   547→3. Check whether the output has enough variation (not 99% one class)\n   548→4. Check that filtering steps for neural activity streams are followed. \n   549→5. Check that processing of the data matches the reference code and paper. \n   550→\n   551→**Iteration protocol**: If ANY check above reveals an issue:\n   552→1. Fix the issue in convert_data.py\n   553→2. Re-run the affected conversion and validation steps\n   554→3. Re-run ALL checks in this step (not just the one that failed)\n   555→4. Document each iteration: what was found, what was fixed, what the re-check showed\n   556→\n   557→**Report all checks and results** to `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` to convince the user that the conversion and decoding has been done correctly. \n   558→\n   559→**Done when**: You have documented your review findings and all issues are resolved in CONVERSION_NOTES.md under \"Step 12\".\n   560→\n   561→---\n   562→\n   563→### Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n   564→\n   565→**Goal**: Finalize documentation and organize files.\n   566→\n   567→**Actions**:\n   568→- Ensure CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete with all:\n   569→  - Key decisions and rationale\n   570→  - Checks for correctness of the conversion\n   571→  - Validation results and tables\n   572→  - Any issues found and how they were resolved\n   573→- Create a user-facing `README.md` summarizing:\n   574→  - Dataset description\n   575→  - How to load and use the converted data\n   576→  - Output format specification\n   577→  - Key statistics\n   578→- Move analysis/investigation scripts to a `cache/` folder\n   579→- Create `cache/README_CACHE.md` documenting cached files\n   580→\n   581→**Done when**: README.md exists, CONVERSION_NOTES.md is complete, and directory is clean.\n   582→\n   583→---\n   584→\n   585→## CONVERSION_NOTES.md Template\n   586→\n   587→**Create this file IMMEDIATELY in Step 0. data\n   839→- **Trial alignment**: Align trials to meaningful events (stimulus onset, movement, etc.)\n   840→- **Time windows**: Choose appropriate pre/post-event windows\n   841→- **Neuron curation**: Choose filtering criteria for the quality of each neuron's recording based on reference texts\n   842→- **Trial curation**: Choose quality filtering criteria for trials based on reference texts or bad or missing data\n   843→\n   844→### Computational Efficiency\n   845→- Be mindful of memory usage with large datasets\n   846→- Use appropriate data types (float32 vs. float64)\n   847→- Consider lazy loading for very large datasets\n   848→- Implement efficient array operations (vectorization)\n   849→\n   850→## Success Criteria\n   851→\n   852→A successful conversion should:\n   853→1. Do this BEFORE exploring any code or data. Document everything in this file as you work.\n\n2. **FOLLOW STEPS IN ORDER**: Do not skip steps. Do not proceed to step N+1 until step N is complete. Each step has \"Done when\" criteria — verify them before moving on.\n\n3. **MATCH THE REFERENCE PROCESSING**: Your processing must match what's described in the reference paper and code. You will be assessed on this consistency.\n\n---\n\n## Project Context\n\n- You are a computational neuroscientist. Your goal is to load and reformat data from a neuroscience paper into a specified structure suitable for downstream analysis.\n- You need to use the **SAME** processing of the data described in the provided reference paper and code repository. \n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your correctness on **matching the loading and processing described in the reference texts and code**. \n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `data`.\n- **Documentation** on nwb file format is at https://nwb-schema.readthedocs.io/en/latest/\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on start of the trial. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n  - 3: 0 cm\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n  - 6: > +50 cm\n- Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Python environment\n\n- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nTo check consistency, you must compare statistics available in the reference paper and your converted dataset. \n\nWhenever possible, invent **SANITY CHECKS** that your loading and processing matches the reference paper and code. \n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above. \n\nCarefully verify your work after every step.  - Reward zones A/B/C are fixed track spans: A `80-130`, B `200-250`, C `320-370` cm.\n   145→  - On switch days, reward zone changes after 30 trials.\n   146→  - ENV2 introduced on day 8; two mice (`m17`, `m18`) started in ENV2.\n   147→- Temporal alignment and sampling:\n   148→  - Neural and behavioral data are analyzed at the imaging frame rate (~15.5 Hz, ~64.5 ms).\n   149→  - Reference code aligns Unity VR data to imaging frames before any later analyses.\n   150→  - For many analyses, only in-trial samples are used; teleport period treatment depends on session/imaging settings.\n   151→- Neural processing:\n   152→  - Fluorescence is motion-corrected and segmented with Suite2P.\n   153→  - dF/F is computed independently within each trial using a maximin baseline with a 20 s window.\n   154→  - dF/F is smoothed with a 2-sample Gaussian.\n   155→  - Deconvolved `events` are extracted with OASIS and are used for decoder and place-cell analyses.\n   156→- Behavioral processing:\n   157→  - Activity at running speeds `<2 cm/s` is excluded from spatial neural analyses and RR decoding.\n   158→  - Licks are binary after correction; corrupted lick trials are set to NaN and excluded from licking analyses.\n   159→  - Reward outcome is trial-specific with random omissions on ~15% of trials.\n   160→- Decoder-specific details:\n   161→  - Target in the paper is circular reward-relative position (`-pi` to `pi`), aligned to reward-zone start.\n   162→  - Tenfold CV, 90% train / 10% test.\n   163→  - Occupancy downsampling matches each RR position bin (`2*pi/45`, about 10 cm).\n   164→  - Shuffles independently circularly shift each neuron’s session timeseries.\n   165→\n   166→### Curation Steps\n   167→\n   168→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   169→- Manual Suite2P curation removes ROIs that:\n   170→  - contain multiple somata or dendrites\n   171→  - lack visually obvious transients\n   172→  - appear overexpressed\n   173→  - show high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n   174→- Additional putative interneurons are excluded if corr(dF/F, running speed) > 0.5.\n   175→- Multi-plane mice pool planes for most analyses.\n   176→\n   177→**Trial curation rules**:\n   178→- Random reward omissions are part of the task and are not discarded by default.\n   179→- Lick-analysis trials are removed when >30% of imaging-frame samples in the trial have cumulative lick count >2.\n   180→- Many analyses exclude samples with running speed <2 cm/s rather than dropping entire trials.\n   181→- Some analyses use:\n   182→  - pre-switch 30 trials\n   183→  - final 30 post-switch trials\n   184→  - first vs second half of trials on non-switch days\n   185→\n   186→### Decoders Trained\n   187→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   188→| Reward-relative position (paper decoder) | No single scalar value stated in text; qualitatively above shuffle for RR cells after switch, with z-scored decode >2 spanning about -104.5 to +152.7 cm around reward |\n   189→\n   190→---\n   191→\n   192→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   193→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   194→\n   195→### Discrepancies Found\n   196→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   197→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   198→| Session container | Code operates on a `sess` object produced earlier, then adds `dff` and `events` | NWB files already store aligned behavior plus `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved` | Paper describes the same pipeline: align VR to imaging, compute dF/F, deconvolve | Treat NWB as an exported `sess`-equivalent. Use NWB aligned behavior streams and map `Deconvolved/plane0/data` to reference `events` unless a later check shows mismatch. |\n   199→| Reward-zone labeling | Code infers reward-zone coordinates from scene names like `Env2_LocationA_to_B` | NWB `reward_zone` samples are not A/B/C labels; values increase within-zone and are only meaningful as `>0` occupancy | Paper defines zone A/B/C by track coordinates and describes scene-based switches | Parse scene/location from NWB `identifier`; use `reward_zone > 0` only as occupancy / entry signal, and derive A/B/C labels from scene metadata for per-trial zone identity. |\n   200→| Trial timing | Code uses `trial_start_inds` / `teleport_inds` and slices `start-1:stop-1` | NWB stores frame-wise `trial_start`, `teleport`, `trial number`, timestamps | Paper defines trials as laps ending in teleport period | Reconstruct trial start/end frame indices from NWB behavior streams so trial segmentation matches the reference semantics. |\n   201→| Environment identity | Code uses `morph` from VR data, with `Env1 -> 0`, `Env2 -> 1` | NWB `environment` contains valid values `0/1` plus pre-sync invalid `-1` | Paper describes two environments ENV1 / ENV2 | Use valid `environment` values directly from NWB after excluding invalid pre-sync samples, and cross-check against scene identifier. |\n   202→| ROI curation count | Code/paper report 155-2172 putative pyramidal neurons/session after curation | NWB `iscell[:,0]==1` gives 155-2341 curated ROIs/session; max session is multi-plane `m18 ses03` | Paper states 155-2172/session and excludes additional putative interneurons by speed correlation | Likely requires additional reference-style interneuron exclusion and possibly careful handling of multi-plane pooled ROIs. This becomes a planned consistency check for conversion. |\n   203→| dF/F availability | Code computes dF/F from `F`/`Fneu` with per-trial maximin baseline | NWB exports `Fluorescence`, `Neuropil`, and `Deconvolved`, but not stored `dff` | Paper explicitly defines dF/F computation and deconvolution | For decoder inputs we mainly need neural activity. Candidate paths are: (1) use NWB `Deconvolved` if it matches reference `events`; (2) recompute dF/F / deconvolution from `Fluorescence` and `Neuropil` if necessary. Need later sanity check against raw NWB signals and paper stats. |\n   204→| Total session count | Task schedule implies 14 days per mouse | NWB has 152 sessions across 11 mice | Paper notes m11 started imaging on day 3 | This matches: 11*14 = 154 planned switch sessions, minus 2 missing early m11 sessions = 152. |\n   205→\n   206→---\n   207→\n   208→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   209→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   210→\n   211→### Variable Mapping\n   212→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   213→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   214→| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Select curated ROIs (`iscell[:,0] == 1`), transpose to `(neurons, time)` per trial, slice `trial_start:teleport` | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Candidate equivalent of reference `sess.timeseries['events']`; later sanity-check against raw NWB. |\n   215→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position` timestamps | `input[0]` = `time_from_trial_start_s` | `timestamps - timestamps[trial_start]` within each trial | Reference alignment is imaging-frame synchronized behavior | Continuous, time-varying, aligned to trial start as required by decoder task. |\n   216→| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment` | `input[1]` = `environment` | Per-trial constant repeated across frames; valid values only (`0/1`) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`morph`) | Cross-check against scene identifier; encode `ENV1=0`, `ENV2=1`. |\n   217→| Reconstructed within-session trial index | `input[2]` = `trial_number` | 0-based per-trial index repeated across frames | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` (`trials`) | Use reconstructed trial order from `trial_start` events, not raw `trial number` during teleport. |\n   218→| Previous trial reward outcome | `input[3]` = `previous_trial_outcome` | For trial `t`, use reward outcome of trial `t-1`; first trial defaults to `0`; repeat across frames | Consistent with task spec; related to `behavior.get_trial_types` | Binary, `0=omitted/unrewarded`, `1=rewarded`. |\n   219→| `position` + trial reward-zone coordinates | `output[0]` = `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, zero inside, positive after zone; discretize into 7 bins from task spec | Paper reward-zone definitions + scene parsing | Use per-trial active zone A/B/C inferred from scene / switch status. |\n   220→| `position` | `output[1]` = `absolute_position_bin` | Bin position on `0-450 cm` track into 5 equal bins | Paper track length 450 cm | Use only in-trial frames; exclude teleport. |\n   221→| `speed` | `output[2]` = `speed_bin` | Discretize cm/s into `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40` | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` speed stream; paper threshold at 2 cm/s | Do not drop low-speed frames because they are a decoder target class here. |\n   222→| `lick` | `output[3]` = `lick` | Clip cumulative lick counts to binary `0/1`; bad-lick trials dropped by QC rule | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`, paper lick QC | Target is time-varying binary lick. |\n   223→| Scene-derived active reward zone | `output[4]` = `reward_zone_location` | Map `A/B/C -> 0/1/2`; repeat across frames in the trial | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | Derived from NWB `identifier` scene string and switch-after-30 rule. |\n   224→| Trial reward delivery from `BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward` | `output[5]` = `reward_outcome` | `1` if any reward event timestamp falls within trial, else `0`; repeat across frames | `behavior.get_trial_types`, `rewardAnalysis.get_reward_inds` | Uses actual delivered reward, matching decoder task. |\n   225→\n   226→### Key Decisions\n   227→1. **Neural signal = deconvolved activity**: The reference decoder and place-cell pipeline use deconvolved calcium activity (`events`). NWB already exports `Deconvolved`, so this is the closest native match.\n   228→2. **Trial-aligned frames exclude teleport period**: Reference code keeps samples from trial start until teleport onset. I will reconstruct trials from `trial_start` impulses to the next `teleport` impulse, excluding teleport frames.\n   229→3. **Use native imaging-frame resolution**: The reference data are sampled at ~15.5 Hz and behavior is already synchronized to this grid. No rebinning in time unless a later validation forces it.\n   230→4. **Per-trial constants will be repeated across time**: Although some decoder variables are per-trial, repeating them across each trial’s time axis gives a uniform `(n_variables, n_timepoints)` representation and matches the decoder’s time-varying format.\n   231→5. **Scene identifier drives reward-zone identity**: NWB `reward_zone` is not the A/B/C label. Active reward-zone identity will be parsed from the session identifier (for example `Env1_LocationA_to_B`, `Env1_B_to_Env2_C`) and the 30-trial switch rule from the paper/code.\n   232→6. **Bad lick trials will be removed**: Because lick is a required decoder output and NaNs are undesirable, trials meeting the paper’s lick-sensor failure rule will be excluded rather than carried with missing labels.\n   233→7. **Initial neuron filter = `iscell[:,0] == 1`**: This matches Suite2p manual curation. Additional interneuron-style exclusions remain a consistency check because of the modest count mismatch with the paper.\n   234→8. **No speed-based sample exclusion in final conversion**: The paper excludes `<2 cm/s` for spatial analyses, but here speed itself is an output class. I will preserve these frames and encode low speed as class `0`.\n   235→9. **Absolute position uses track-only interval**: Position bins will cover the 450 cm corridor only; teleport frames are excluded, so no teleport-specific bins are needed.\n   236→\n   237→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   238→- [ ] Check 1: For a chosen session/trial, verify `neural[session][trial]` equals the raw NWB `Deconvolved` samples at the same curated ROI indices and frame range with `np.allclose`.\n   239→- [ ] Check 2: For chosen trials, verify reconstructed trial start/end frames match raw `trial_start` / `teleport` impulse locations and that converted time starts at 0 s.\n   240→- [ ] Check 3: For chosen trials, verify raw `position` reproduces `absolute_position_bin` and `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` exactly under the planned binning rule.\n   241→- [ ] Check 4: Verify per-trial reward outcome from converted data matches presence/absence of raw `Reward` events within the raw frame/timestamp interval.\n   242→- [ ] Check 5: Verify scene parsing and 30-trial switch logic reproduce per-trial reward-zone labels consistent with paper/code examples.\n   243→\n  379→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   380→\n   381→### Checks Performed\n   382→1. **Output log verification**: `verification_full_out.txt` reports no errors and no warnings.\n   383→2. **Sanity checks against raw NWB**:\n   384→   - Single-plane spot check (`session 0`, `trial 10`): raw deconvolved activity vs converted neural matched with `np.allclose(..., atol=1e-3)`.\n   385→   - Multi-plane spot check (`session 84`, `trial 5`, `m18 ses-03`): raw plane0+plane1 reconstruction in segmentation-table order vs converted neural matched with `np.allclose(..., atol=1e-3)`.\n   386→   - Input position/speed/lick/reward-derived outputs matched converted outputs exactly.\n   388→   - Switch edge case check (`sub-m11_ses-08_behavior+ophys.nwb`): trial 29 resolves to `(Env1, B)` and trial 30 resolves to `(Env2, C)`, confirming cross-environment switch parsing.\n   389→3. **Reference code comparison**:\n   390→   - `(a) data loading`: reference loads a `sess` object; converter loads the NWB export directly. This is a packaging difference, not a semantic one.\n   391→   - `(b) neuron/trial filtering`: converter applies `iscell` and lick-trial QC; reference also mentions a speed-correlation interneuron exclusion. A deconvolved-speed proxy check on the largest session found 0 neurons above `r > 0.5`, so this is not the source of the main count mismatch.\n   392→   - `(c) temporal alignment`: converter uses `trial_start -> teleport`, matching the reference in-trial slicing logic.\n   393→   - `(d) binning`: converter keeps the native ~15.5 Hz time base and applies task-required output discretization; paper/reference spatial binning is 10 cm, used here only where needed for target outputs.\n   394→   - `(e) input construction`: time, env, trial number, and previous outcome are built from raw aligned behavior and repeated across frames.\n   395→   - `(f) output construction`: outputs are derived directly from raw behavior plus scene-defined reward zones and checked against raw NWB values.\n   396→4. **Key statistics comparison**:\n   397→   - Raw NWB reconstructed reward rate is `0.8466`, omission rate `0.1534`, matching the paper’s approximately 15% omission design.\n   398→   - Raw reconstructed trials from `trial_start`/`teleport` total `12,216`; converted data keep `12,147` after dropping `69` lick-error trials (`0.565%`), close to the paper’s reported `81/12,376 = 0.654%`.\n   399→   - Raw curated ROI count range is `155` to `2341`; converted data preserve the same range.\n   400→5. **Edge-case checks**:\n   401→   - `sub-m11_ses-03` has `81` nonnegative trial numbers but only `80` `trial_start` and `teleport` events. Converter follows `trial_start`/`teleport` and therefore excludes one partial/non-aligned trial, which is consistent with trial-start alignment.\n   402→   - Multi-plane sessions (`m17`, `m18`) required explicit reconstruction from `plane0` and `plane1`; this was fixed before the full conversion.\n   403→\n   404→### Issues Found and Resolved\n   405→- **Multi-plane ROI loading bug**: Initial converter assumed a single `plane0` response matrix and failed on `m17/m18`. Fixed by reconstructing a full session matrix from `plane0` and `plane1` using `planeIdx`, then applying `iscell` curation in segmentation-table order. Re-ran sample and full conversion after the fix.\n   406→- **Paper-vs-release trial count mismatch**: Paper quote (`12,376` trials) does not match raw NWB reconstructed trials (`12,216`). This discrepancy is present in the released data itself and is not introduced by conversion.\n   407→- **Paper-vs-release max neuron count mismatch**: Paper reports up to `2172` neurons/session, while raw NWB has one multi-plane session with `2341` curated ROIs. This discrepancy is present in the released data itself; converted data preserve the release values.\n   408→\n   409→---\n   410→\n   411→## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   412→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   413→\n   414→### Training Progress\n   415→- Loss decreasing: Yes\n   416→\n   417→### Decoder Results (Full)\n   418→| Output | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Notes |\n   419→|--------|-------------|--------|-------|\n   420→| `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` | 0.4742 | 0.4272 | Well above chance (0.1429) |\n   421→| `absolute_position_bin` | 0.5374 | 0.5108 | Well above chance (0.2000) |\n   422→| `speed_bin` | 0.4346 | 0.4141 | Well above chance (0.2000) |\n   423→| `lick` | 0.5917 | 0.5847 | Above chance (0.5000) but smallest margin |\n   424→| `reward_zone_location` | 0.8433 | 0.8129 | Strongly above chance (0.3333) |\n   425→| `reward_outcome` | 0.5759 | 0.5281 | Slightly above chance (0.5000) |\n   426→\n   427→---\n   428→\n   429→## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   430→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   431→\n   432→### Accuracy Analysis\n   433→\n   434→| Variable | Achieved Accuracy | Expectation from Paper |\n   435→| `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` | 0.4272 validation balanced accuracy | Paper does not report this exact target; should be clearly above chance if alignment is correct |\n   436→| `absolute_position_bin` | 0.5108 | Paper does not report this exact target |\n   437→| `speed_bin` | 0.4141 | Paper does not report this exact target |\n   438→| `lick` | 0.5847 | Paper does not report this exact target |\n   439→| `reward_zone_location` | 0.8129 | Paper does not report this exact target; `reward_outcome` is `15.81% no / 84.19% yes`, so neither output is degenerate.\n   449→  - Train/validation ratios are small (`lick 1.012`, `reward_outcome 1.091`), arguing against strong overfitting or leakage.\n   450→  - Temporal alignment had already been checked visually in processing plots and quantitatively in Step 10 sanity checks.\n   451→- Interpretation:\n   452→  - `reward_outcome` is encoded as a per-trial label repeated across time, per the decoder task spec. Raw imaging + VR -> `sess` via `create_sess` / `append_session_data`.\n  2. `sess.align_VR_to_2P()` aligns Unity VR to imaging frame times.\n  3. Behavior timeseries added directly from aligned VR (`licks`, `rewards`, `speed`).\n  4. dF/F is not stored in the raw `sess`; it is computed later by `multi_anim_sess` using `preprocessing.dff`.\n  5. Deconvolved calcium `events` are derived from dF/F and used for the decoder notebook.\n- The code is for 2-photon calcium imaging, not electrophysiology.\n- Delta F over F does need to be computed in the original analysis pipeline.\n- Cell curation is not an ephys spike-quality filter. Instead, ROIs are manually curated in the Suite2p GUI, which updates `iscell.npy`; `sess.load_suite2p_data()` loads that curation.\n- `multi_anim_sess_README.md` states each sample is one imaging frame at about 15.5 Hz (~64.5 ms/sample), and `trial_matrices` are spatial bins of 10 cm across 0-450 cm.\n- Decoder-relevant continuous variables in reference code:\n  - neural: `sess.timeseries['events']`\n  - position: `sess.vr_data['pos']`\n  - reward-relative position: computed from reward-zone start position per trial\n  - trial id: repeated sample-wise from trial index\n  - speed: `sess.timeseries['speed']`\n  - rewards: `sess.timeseries['rewards']`\n  - licks: `sess.timeseries['licks']`, with values >1 clipped to 1 and invalid sensors masked\n- Important masking/alignment details from `get_timeseries_data`:\n  - Only samples from `trial_start_inds` to `teleport_inds` are kept.\n  - Trial slices use `start-1:stop-1`, so reference indexing is effectively 1-based for stored trial boundary indices.\n  - Samples with NaNs in `events`, low speed (`<2 cm/s` by default), or invalid lick samples are removed for the paper’s GLM/decoder analyses.\n  - Reward and omission signals are represented as state variables that switch after reward delivery or reward-zone entry, respectively, within a trial.\n- The paper’s Figure 3 decoder notebook decodes circular reward-relative position from deconvolved events, and down-samples observations to match position occupancy across trial sets before training.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset values `0..8` observed in sample files\n  - `iscell`: shape `(n_rois, 2)` in sample files, consistent with Suite2p export `[is_cell_flag, probability]`\n- Example session (`sub-m3_ses-01`):\n  - -104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Task structure:\n  - 450 cm linear VR track with hidden 50 cm reward zone.\n  - Reward zones A/B/C are fixed track spans: A `80-130`, B `200-250`, C `320-370` cm.\n  - On switch days, reward zone changes after 30 trials.\n  - ENV2 introduced on day 8; two mice (`m17`, `m18`) started in ENV2.\n- Temporal alignment and sampling:\n  - Neural and behavioral data are analyzed at the imaging frame rate (~15.5 Hz, ~64.5 ms).\n  - Reference code aligns Unity VR data to imaging frames before any later analyses.\n  - For many analyses, only in-trial samples are used; teleport period treatment depends on session/imaging settings.\n- Neural processing:\n  - Fluorescence is motion-corrected and segmented with Suite2P.\n  - dF/F is computed independently within each trial using a maximin baseline with a 20 s window.\n  - dF/F is smoothed with a 2-sample Gaussian.\n  - Deconvolved `events` are extracted with OASIS and are used for decoder and place-cell analyses.\n- Behavioral processing:\n  - Activity at running speeds `<2 cm/s` is excluded from spatial neural analyses and RR decoding.\n  - Licks are binary after correction; corrupted lick trials are set to NaN and excluded from licking analyses.\n  - Reward outcome is trial-specific with random omissions on ~15% of trials.\n- Decoder-specific details:\n  - Target in the paper is circular reward-relative position (`-pi` to `pi`), aligned to reward-zone start.\n  - Tenfold CV, 90% train / 10% test.\n  - Occupancy downsampling matches each RR position bin (`2*pi/45`, about 10 cm).\n  - Shuffles independently circularly shift each neuron’s session timeseries.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Manual Suite2P curation removes ROIs that:\n  - contain multiple somata or dendrites\n  - lack visually obvious transients\n  - appear overexpressed\n  - show high continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\n- Additional putative interneurons are excluded if corr(dF/F, running speed) > 0.5.\n- Multi-plane mice pool planes for most analyses.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Random reward omissions are part of the task and are not discarded by default.\n- Lick-analysis trials are removed when >30% of imaging-frame samples in the trial have cumulative lick count >2.\n- Many analyses exclude samples with running speed <2 cm/s rather than dropping entire trials.\n- Some analyses use:\n  - pre-switch 30 trials\n  - final 30 post-switch trials\n  - first vs second half of trials on non-switch days\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Reward-relative position (paper decoder) | No single scalar value stated in text; qualitatively above shuffle for RR cells after switch, with z-scored decode >2 spanning about -104.5 to +152.7 cm around reward |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Candidate paths are: (1) use NWB `Deconvolved` if it matches reference `events`; (2) recompute dF/F / deconvolution from `Fluorescence` and `Neuropil` if necessary. Need later sanity check against raw NWB signals and paper stats. |\n| Total session count | Task schedule implies 14 days per mouse | NWB has 152 sessions across 11 mice | Paper notes m11 started imaging on day 3 | This matches: 11*14 = 154 planned switch sessions, minus 2 missing early m11 sessions = 152. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data` | `neural` | Select curated ROIs (`iscell[:,0] == 1`), transpose to `(neurons, time)` per trial, slice `trial_start:teleport` | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`, `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | Candidate equivalent of reference `sess.timeseries['events']`; later sanity-check against raw NWB. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position` timestamps | `input[0]` = `time_from_trial_start_s` | `timestamps - timestamps[trial_start]` within each trial | Reference alignment is imaging-frame synchronized behavior | Continuous, time-varying, aligned to trial start as required by decoder task. |\n| `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment` | `input[1]` = `environment` | Per-trial constant repeated across frames; valid values only (`0/1`) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`morph`) | Cross-check against scene identifier; encode `ENV1=0`, `ENV2=1`. |\n| Reconstructed within-session trial index | `input[2]` = `trial_number` | 0-based per-trial index repeated across frames | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` (`trials`) | Use reconstructed trial order from `trial_start` events, not raw `trial number` during teleport. |\n| Previous trial reward outcome | `input[3]` = `previous_trial_outcome` | For trial `t`, use reward outcome of trial `t-1`; first trial defaults to `0`; repeat across frames | Consistent with task spec; related to `behavior.get_trial_types` | Binary, `0=omitted/unrewarded`, `1=rewarded`. |\n| `position` + trial reward-zone coordinates | `output[0]` = `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` | Signed distance to nearest point in active reward zone: negative before zone, zero inside, positive after zone; discretize into 7 bins from task spec | Paper reward-zone definitions + scene parsing | Use per-trial active zone A/B/C inferred from scene / switch status. |\n| `position` | `output[1]` = `absolute_position_bin` | Bin position on `0-450 cm` track into 5 equal bins | Paper track length 450 cm | Use only in-trial frames; exclude teleport. |\n| `speed` | `output[2]` = `speed_bin` | Discretize cm/s into `<2`, `2-10`, `10-20`, `20-40`, `>40` | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` speed stream; paper threshold at 2 cm/s | Do not drop low-speed frames because they are a decoder target class here. |\n| `lick` | `output[3]` = `lick` | Clip cumulative lick counts to binary `0/1`; bad-lick trials dropped by QC rule | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`, paper lick QC | Target is time-varying binary lick. |\n| Scene-derived active reward zone | `output[4]` = `reward_zone_location` | Map `A/B/C -> 0/1/2`; repeat across frames in the trial | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | Derived from NWB `identifier` scene string and switch-after-30 rule. |\n| Trial reward delivery from `BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward` | `output[5]` = `reward_outcome` | `1` if any reward event timestamp falls within trial, else `0`; repeat across frames | `behavior.get_trial_types`, `rewardAnalysis.get_reward_inds` | Uses actual delivered reward, matching decoder task. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neural signal = deconvolved activity**: The reference decoder and place-cell pipeline use deconvolved calcium activity (`events`). NWB already exports `Deconvolved`, so this is the closest native match.\n2. **Trial-aligned frames exclude teleport period**: Reference code keeps samples from trial start until teleport onset. I will reconstruct trials from `trial_start` impulses to the next `teleport` impulse, excluding teleport frames.\n3. **Use native imaging-frame resolution**: The reference data are sampled at ~15.5 Hz and behavior is already synchronized to this grid. No rebinning in time unless a later validation forces it.\n4. **Per-trial constants will be repeated across time**: Although some decoder variables are per-trial, repeating them across each trial’s time axis gives a uniform `(n_variables, n_timepoints)` representation and matches the decoder’s time-varying format.\n5. **Scene identifier drives reward-zone identity**: NWB `reward_zone` is not the A/B/C label. Active reward-zone identity will be parsed from the session identifier (for example `Env1_LocationA_to_B`, `Env1_B_to_Env2_C`) and the 30-trial switch rule from the paper/code.\n6. **Bad lick trials will be removed**: Because lick is a required decoder output and NaNs are undesirable, trials meeting the paper’s lick-sensor failure rule will be excluded rather than carried with missing labels.\n7. **Initial neuron filter = `iscell[:,0] == 1`**: This matches Suite2p manual curation. Additional interneuron-style exclusions remain a consistency check because of the modest count mismatch with the paper.\n8. **No speed-based sample exclusion in final conversion**: The paper excludes `<2 cm/s` for spatial analyses, but here speed itself is an output class. I will preserve these frames and encode low speed as class `0`.\n9. **Absolute position uses track-only interval**: Position bins will cover the 450 cm corridor only; teleport frames are excluded, so no teleport-specific bins are needed.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Check 1: For a chosen session/trial, verify `neural[session][trial]` equals the raw NWB `Deconvolved` samples at the same curated ROI indices and frame range with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Check 2: For chosen trials, verify reconstructed trial start/end frames match raw `trial_start` / `teleport` impulse locations and that converted time starts at 0 s.\n- [ ] Check 3: For chosen trials, verify raw `position` reproduces `absolute_position_bin` and `distance_to_reward_zone_bin` exactly under the planned binning rule.\n- [ ] Check 4: Verify per-trial reward outcome from converted data matches presence/absence of raw `Reward` events within the raw frame/timestamp interval.\n- [ ] Check 5: Verify scene parsing and 30-trial switch logic reproduce per-trial reward-zone labels consistent with paper/code examples.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Implemented `convert_data.py` with CLI:\n  - `python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile>`\n  - `--full`\n  - `--sample`\n  - `--show-processing`\n- Current implementation choices:\n  - Reads NWB directly with `h5py`\n  - Uses NWB `Deconvolved/plane0/data` as neural activity\n  - Filters ROIs with `iscell[:,0] == 1`\n  - Reconstructs trials from `trial_start` impulse to `teleport` impulse\n  - Drops trials failing lick-sensor QC\n  - Derives environment and reward-zone identity from the NWB `identifier` scene string and the 30-trial switch rule\n  - Repeats per-trial inputs/outputs across frames so all trials are stored as peak memory bounded by one session.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons In their rawest form, they do NOT contain dF/F (dFF).\\n   1. dFF and trial_matrices (spatially binned data) can be added separately via methods of the sess class\\n   2. See make_session_pkl.ipynb\\n   3. Original code to construct the sess lives in the TwoPUtils repo\\n2. `multi_anim_sess`: computes dFF, calculates place cells, adds details like a trial set dictionary, and collects these and the sess data for multiple animals on a single day. Useful so you can work off a constant set of place cell IDs (since place cells are identified by their spatial information relative to a shuffle, which is stochastic for each run of the shuffle for cells that are borderline significant).\\n   1. See the [multi_anim_sess README](docs/multi_anim_sess_README.md) for a detailed description.\\n   1. See also `./notebooks/make_multi_anim_sess.ipynb`\\n3. `dayData`: class that takes multi_anim_sess as an input and performs additional computations like finding place cell peaks, computing circular distance between peaks relative to reward, computing correlation matrices, etc.\\n   1. Original sess data are not re-saved here, but a copy of the trial matrices are kept.\\n   2. `dayData.py` lives in the reward_relative modules\\n   3. Use `./notebooks/make_multiDayData_class.ipynb` to generate this class and save it as a pickle.\\n\\n## Preprocessing guide\\n\\n[Order of operations for running preprocessing](docs/preprocessing_guide.md)\\n\\n### Using jupytext for .ipynb version control\\n\\nJupyter notebooks are great for debugging and plotting, but sharing jupyter notebooks or merging them across branches with git can be a mess (because of the outputs). \\n\\n[Jupytext](https://jupytext.readthedocs.io/en/latest/install.html) is a useful tool that we can use to synchronize jupyter notebooks to markdown (.md) files for vastly improved version control. Edits can now be easily compared and merged across branches, for instance, because notebook outputs and formatting are excluded or converted to plain text. This is also very useful if you are trying out my notebooks but having trouble loading the .ipynb in your IDE of choice -- the .md file will let you start fresh with the same code, but without any saved outputs or cell runs.\\n\\n1. To install jupytext in your virtual environment:\\n```\\npip install jupytext --upgrade\\n```\\n\\n2. To synchronize a .ipynb with a .md:\\n```\\njupytext --set-formats ipynb,md --sync notebook.ipynb\\n```\\n\\n3. If you come across a .md without a .ipynb or you want to start fresh from the .md, just synchronize in the other direction:\\n```\\njupytext --set-formats ipynb,md --sync notebook.md\\n```\\n\\n\\n## A note about stochasticity in exact results\\n\\nMany of our computations, including thresholds for classifying individual cells, rely on shuffles or other randomized functions. For instance, when determining which cells are place cells, we compare the spatial information of each cell to the distribution of spatial information scores within the animal resulting from 100 shuffles per cell of position relative to neural activity. Since the shuffle is randomly generated each time the code is run, there will always be +/- a few cells that pass as place cells/not place cells each time, for the cells that have borderline significant spatial information (consider the case where a cell has p=0.04 on one run of the shuffle and p=0.05 on another run). A similar type of shuffle criterion is used to define reward-relative cells. \\n\\nThis results in some stochasticity, such that if a user runs the whole pipeline from raw or early-level pre-processed data to plotting figures, they will not necessarily generate the __exact__ same plot that is in the paper. Importantly, however, the overall result should be the same, just perhaps with a slightly different n of included cells or slightly different p-value (but same order of magnitude) from the result reported in the paper, as the paper results came from a particular iteration of all of these randomized computations. We view this as a positive scientifically, because population-level results do not depend on the inclusion or exclusion of a handful of cells. \\n\\nTo replicate the plots exactly as they are in the paper, the post-processed version of the data used for the manuscript will be provided. \\n\"\n   357→          }\n   358→        ]\n   359→      },\n   360→      \"extra\": {\n   361→        \"raw_arguments\": \"{\\\"cmd\\\":\\\"sed -n '1,220p' code/README.md\\\",\\\"workdir\\\":\\\"/app\\\",\\\"yield_time_ms\\\":1000,\\\"max_output_tokens\\\":4000}\"\n   362→      }\n   363→    },\n   364→    {"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_016PSGiwS7GriDyhWhavYNC8","session_id":"29cc29ba-b017-4d59-9e66-b9787d9e9438","uuid":"a62e7f3a-5060-452b-9285-417f38c3667a","timestamp":"2026-08-03T16:39:31.526Z"}
